## Chapter 17: AHC Leaders & Teams Management

*Managing leadership profiles and team member information*

This chapter covers the recently implemented Leaders & Teams feature, which allows you to manage both AHC leaders and team members in a unified system.

### 17.1 Understanding Leaders vs. Teams

The AHC Leaders & Teams system differentiates between two types of people:

**AHC Leaders:**
- Executive leadership
- Board members
- Directors and senior management
- Key decision-makers
- Display on: /ahcleaders page

**AHC Team Members:**
- Staff members
- Program coordinators
- Research associates
- Support staff
- Display on: /ahc-team page

Both are managed in the same backend interface but display separately on the frontend.

**Key Difference:**
- **Type field** determines where they appear
- Same management interface
- Similar data structure
- Different frontend pages

### 17.2 Creating a New Leader or Team Member

**Navigation:**
1. Sidebar: People & Organizations > AHC Leaders & Teams
2. Click "Add New" button

**Step-by-Step Creation:**

**1. Select Type (First Card)**

Critical first step:
- **Type dropdown:**
  - ☐ AHC Leader
  - ☐ AHC Team Member
- Choose based on their role
- Determines which page they appear on
- Can be changed later if needed

**2. Basic Information**

**Full Name:**
- First and last name
- Example: "Dr. Jane Smith"
- Used in displays, cards, search

**Position/Title:**
- Job title or role
- Example: "Executive Director" or "Research Coordinator"
- Appears below name on cards

**3. Photo Upload**

**Profile Photo:**
- Click "Upload Photo" or drag-and-drop
- Requirements:
  - Format: JPG or PNG
  - Aspect ratio: 1:1 (square) preferred or 3:4 (portrait)
  - Minimum: 400×400 pixels
  - Recommended: 800×800 pixels
  - Max file size: 2MB
- Guidelines:
  - Professional headshot
  - Clear, well-lit face
  - Neutral background
  - Appropriate attire
  - Smile recommended
  - Eye-level camera angle

**Photo Tips:**
- Consistent style across all leaders/team
- Same background or style
- Similar lighting
- Professional photographer recommended
- Update periodically (every 1-2 years)

**4. Biography**

**Rich Text Editor:**
- Write detailed biography
- Structure suggestions:
  - **Background:** Education, experience
  - **Current Role:** Responsibilities at AHC
  - **Expertise:** Areas of specialization
  - **Achievements:** Key accomplishments
  - **Research Interests:** Current focus (for researchers)
  - **Personal:** Optional humanizing detail

**Length Recommendations:**
- Leaders: 150-300 words
- Team Members: 100-200 words

**Writing Tips:**
- Third person ("Dr. Smith leads..." not "I lead...")
- Professional but personable tone
- Highlight relevant experience
- Include key achievements
- Avoid jargon
- Proofread carefully

**Example Biography:**

```
Dr. Jane Smith serves as Executive Director of the Africa Health 
Collaborative, bringing over 15 years of experience in public health 
systems strengthening. She holds a PhD in Health Policy from Harvard 
University and has worked extensively across East Africa.

Prior to joining AHC, Dr. Smith led health systems research at the 
World Health Organization and served as Senior Health Advisor for 
the Ethiopian Ministry of Health. Her research focuses on health 
workforce development and community health systems.

Dr. Smith has published over 40 peer-reviewed articles and co-authored 
the widely-used Health Systems Toolkit. She is passionate about building 
local capacity and sustainable health solutions.
```

**5. LinkedIn URL (Optional)**

**LinkedIn Profile:**
- Full LinkedIn profile URL
- Example: `https://www.linkedin.com/in/janesmith`
- Appears as clickable icon on profile
- Opens in new tab

**How to Find:**
1. Go to person's LinkedIn profile
2. Copy URL from address bar
3. Paste entire URL (including https://)

**Validation:**
- Must be valid URL
- Must start with http:// or https://
- Max 500 characters

**Why Include:**
- Professional networking
- Additional credentials
- Extended bio
- Recommendations visible
- Publication links
- Builds credibility

**6. Display Order**

**Order Number:**
- Controls display sequence
- Lower numbers appear first
- Example: 1, 2, 3, 4...
- Leave gaps for easy insertion (10, 20, 30...)

**Ordering Strategy:**

**For Leaders:**
- Hierarchical order:
  1. Executive Director
  2. Deputy Directors
  3. Program Directors
  4. Department Heads
- Or alphabetical by last name

**For Team Members:**
- By department
- By seniority
- Alphabetical
- By join date

**7. Status**

**Publication Status:**
- **Published:** Visible on website
- **Draft:** Hidden, work in progress
- **Archived:** Removed from display but preserved

**Use Cases:**
- Draft: Person joining soon, prepare profile early
- Published: Active leader/team member
- Archived: Former member, maintain for records

**8. Save**

- Click "Create" button
- Success message appears
- Redirected to all leaders/teams list
- Can view on frontend immediately

### 17.3 The Leaders & Teams Datatable

**Accessing the List:**
- Navigate to: People & Organizations > AHC Leaders & Teams > All Leaders & Teams

**Datatable Columns:**

1. **Photo** - Thumbnail of profile photo
2. **Name** - Full name (clickable to edit)
3. **Position** - Job title
4. **Type** - Badge showing "Leader" (blue) or "Team" (cyan)
5. **LinkedIn** - Icon if LinkedIn URL provided
6. **Order** - Display order number
7. **Status** - Published, Draft, or Archived
8. **Actions** - View, Edit, Delete buttons

**Datatable Features:**

**Search:**
- Search by name or position
- Real-time filtering
- Searches across all fields

**Filters:**

**Type Filter:**
- All Types (default)
- Leaders only
- Team Members only
- Quick way to view each group

**Status Filter:**
- All Statuses
- Published
- Draft
- Archived

**Date Range Filter:**
- Filter by creation date
- Filter by last modified date

**Sorting:**
- Click column headers to sort
- Sort by: Name, Position, Type, Order, Status
- Ascending/descending

**Bulk Actions:**
- Select multiple (checkboxes)
- Bulk delete
- Bulk change status
- Bulk change type (Leader ↔ Team)

**Quick Actions:**
- **👁️ View:** Preview profile on frontend
- **✏️ Edit:** Modify details
- **🗑️ Delete:** Remove (confirms first)

### 17.4 Editing Leaders and Team Members

**To Edit:**
1. Find person in datatable
2. Click name or Edit button
3. Make changes
4. Click "Update" to save

**Common Edits:**

**Updating Photo:**
1. Remove old photo (click X)
2. Upload new photo
3. Follow same photo guidelines
4. Save

**Updating Biography:**
1. Edit in rich text editor
2. Add recent achievements
3. Update title if changed
4. Save

**Changing Type:**
1. Open Type dropdown
2. Select different type (Leader ↔ Team)
3. Save
4. Person moves to other page on frontend

**Adding LinkedIn:**
1. Get person's LinkedIn URL
2. Paste in LinkedIn URL field
3. Validate URL is correct
4. Save
5. Icon appears on their profile card

**Reordering:**
1. Change Order number
2. Or use drag-and-drop (if available)
3. Save
4. View frontend to verify new order

### 17.5 Reordering Leaders and Teams

Display order matters for visual hierarchy.

**Method 1: Order Field**
1. Edit person
2. Change Order number
3. Save
4. Repeat for others as needed

**Method 2: Drag-and-Drop (if available)**
1. View all leaders/teams list
2. Look for drag handle icon (⋮⋮)
3. Drag person up or down
4. Drop in new position
5. Click "Save Order"
6. Changes reflect on frontend

**Best Practices:**

**For Leaders Page:**
- Order by organizational hierarchy
- Executive Director first (order: 1)
- Directors next (order: 2, 3, 4...)
- Department heads follow
- Or alphabetical for peer levels

**For Team Page:**
- Group by department
- Senior staff before junior
- Or full alphabetical
- Or by join date (seniority)

**Pro Tip:**
- Use increments of 10 (10, 20, 30...)
- Makes insertion easier
- Example: Insert between 20 and 30 → use 25

### 17.6 Frontend Display

**Leaders Page (/ahcleaders):**

**Layout:**
- Grid of leader cards
- Filters by type='leader' automatically
- Displays in order specified

**Leader Card Shows:**
- Profile photo (circular crop)
- Full name
- Position/title
- LinkedIn icon (if URL provided)
  - Blue LinkedIn icon top-right
  - Clickable, opens profile in new tab
- Click card → goes to detail page

**Call-to-Action:**
- Bottom of page: "Meet Our AHC Team" button
- Links to /ahc-team page

**Team Page (/ahc-team):**

**Layout:**
- Similar grid layout
- Filters by type='team' automatically
- Displays in order specified

**Team Card Shows:**
- Same as leader cards
- Profile photo (circular)
- Name, position
- LinkedIn icon if provided

**Call-to-Action:**
- Bottom of page: "Back to AHC Leaders" button
- Links back to /ahcleaders

**Detail Page:**

Clicking any leader or team member card opens detail page:

**URL Structure:**
- /ahcleaders/[slug] (e.g., /ahcleaders/dr-jane-smith)
- Slug auto-generated from name

**Detail Page Shows:**
- Large profile photo (left side or top)
- Full name (H1 heading)
- Position (below name)
- LinkedIn button (if URL provided)
  - "View LinkedIn Profile" button
  - LinkedIn blue color (#0A66C2)
  - Opens in new tab
- Full biography (formatted)
- Back button:
  - "Back to AHC Leaders" (if type=leader)
  - "Back to AHC Team" (if type=team)

**Dark Mode Support:**
- All pages support dark mode
- Automatic switching based on user preference
- Maintains readability and contrast

### 17.7 LinkedIn Integration

**How It Works:**

**Backend:**
1. Admin enters LinkedIn URL when creating/editing profile
2. System validates URL format
3. Stores full URL in database

**Frontend Display:**

**On Leader/Team Cards:**
- LinkedIn icon appears top-right corner of photo
- Blue LinkedIn logo (#0A66C2)
- Positioned absolutely over photo
- Hover effect (slight scale/shadow)
- Click → opens LinkedIn profile in new tab

**On Detail Page:**
- "View LinkedIn Profile" button
- LinkedIn icon + text
- Below position title
- Same blue color
- Opens in new tab

**Benefits:**
- Professional networking
- Extended credentials
- Real-time updates (person updates own LinkedIn)
- Recommendations visible
- Publication links
- Builds trust and credibility

**Privacy:**
- Optional field
- Person can decline to provide
- Can remove anytime
- No data scraped from LinkedIn
- Just a link to their public profile

### 17.8 Managing Former Leaders/Team Members

When someone leaves:

**Option 1: Archive**
1. Edit their profile
2. Change Status to "Archived"
3. Save
4. They disappear from frontend
5. Still in database for records
6. Can restore if they return

**Option 2: Update Biography**
1. Keep as Published
2. Update position to "Former [Title]"
3. Update bio: Add "Dr. Smith served as Executive Director from 2020-2025..."
4. Move to bottom of order (order: 999)
5. Shows in "Former Leaders" section (if you create one)

**Option 3: Delete**
1. Only if absolutely no need to keep record
2. Confirms before permanent deletion
3. Cannot be undone
4. Photo and all data removed

**Recommended: Option 1 (Archive)**
- Preserves history
- Easy to restore
- Maintains data integrity
- Can reference later

### 17.9 Team Management Best Practices

✅ **Photo Consistency:**
- Same photographer for all
- Same background or style
- Same lighting setup
- Same aspect ratio
- Professional quality
- Update all when style changes

✅ **Bio Quality:**
- Proofread carefully
- Fact-check credentials
- Update regularly (annual review)
- Consistent tone and length
- Third-person voice
- Highlight AHC-relevant experience

✅ **LinkedIn:**
- Encourage all to provide
- Verify URLs work
- Update if person changes LinkedIn URL
- Respect those who decline

✅ **Order & Organization:**
- Clear, logical ordering
- Document ordering principle
- Review when org structure changes
- Consistent across leaders and team

✅ **Maintenance:**
- Quarterly review
- Update positions/titles when changed
- Add new hires promptly
- Archive departures
- Refresh photos every 1-2 years
- Update bios with new achievements

### 17.10 Common Workflows

**Workflow 1: Adding New Executive Director**

1. Navigate to Add New Leader
2. Type: AHC Leader
3. Name: Dr. John Doe
4. Position: Executive Director
5. Upload professional headshot (800×800px)
6. Write biography (250 words)
   - Background
   - Vision for AHC
   - Previous experience
   - Key achievements
7. LinkedIn: https://linkedin.com/in/johndoe
8. Order: 1 (appears first)
9. Status: Published
10. Save
11. Verify on /ahcleaders page
12. Announce via news article
13. Update other leaders' order if needed

**Workflow 2: Adding Multiple Team Members**

1. Prepare all materials:
   - Photos (same photographer session)
   - Bio text for each
   - LinkedIn URLs
2. Add first team member:
   - Type: Team Member
   - Name, position, photo, bio, LinkedIn
   - Order: 10
   - Status: Published
3. Repeat for each team member
   - Increment order: 20, 30, 40...
4. Review all on /ahc-team page
5. Check ordering, appearance
6. Announce new team via news article or social media

**Workflow 3: Reorganizing Leadership Display**

1. Review current order on /ahcleaders
2. Determine new hierarchy:
   - Executive Director (1)
   - Deputy Directors (2, 3)
   - Program Directors (4, 5, 6)
3. Edit each leader, update Order field
4. Save each
5. Clear cache if needed
6. Verify new order on frontend
7. Document ordering principle for future reference

**Workflow 4: Archiving Former Team Member**

1. Person resigns/departs
2. Navigate to their profile
3. Edit
4. Option A: Archive
   - Change Status to "Archived"
   - Save
   - Removed from frontend immediately
5. Option B: Mark as Former
   - Update Position: "Former Program Coordinator"
   - Update bio: "Jane served as..."
   - Change Order to high number (900)
   - Keep Published
6. Announce departure (if appropriate) via news
7. Recruit and add replacement

## Chapter 18: Partners Management

*Managing organizational partnerships and collaborators*

### 18.1 Understanding Partners

Partners are organizations that collaborate with AHC on research, programs, funding, or other initiatives.

**Types of Partners:**

- **Academic Institutions** - Universities, research centers
- **Government Agencies** - Ministries, health departments
- **NGOs & Civil Society** - Non-profit organizations
- **International Organizations** - WHO, UN agencies, foundations
- **Private Sector** - Companies, corporations
- **Funders & Donors** - Funding organizations
- **Community Organizations** - Grassroots groups, CBOs

### 18.2 Creating a Partner

**Navigation:**
1. Sidebar: People & Organizations > Partners
2. Click "Add New Partner"

**Partner Information:**

**1. Basic Details**

**Partner Name:**
- Official organization name
- Example: "World Health Organization"
- Used in all displays

**Partner Type:**
- Select from dropdown
- Academic Institution
- Government Agency
- NGO
- International Organization
- Private Sector
- Funder/Donor
- Community Organization
- Other

**Short Description:**
- One-sentence summary
- 150 characters max
- Example: "Leading global health organization"

**Full Description:**
- Detailed partnership information
- What they do
- Partnership focus areas
- Collaboration history
- Joint projects
- 200-500 words

**2. Logo Upload**

**Partner Logo:**
- Upload organization logo
- Format: PNG (with transparency) preferred, or JPG
- Aspect ratio: Varies (maintain original)
- Recommended: 600px wide
- Max file size: 2MB
- Transparent background preferred

**Logo Guidelines:**
- Official brand logo
- High resolution
- Clean, uncluttered
- Proper aspect ratio
- Brand colors intact

**3. Contact Information**

**Website URL:**
- Partner's official website
- Full URL with https://
- Example: https://www.who.int

**Email:**
- Official contact email
- General or partnership-specific
- Example: partnerships@organization.org

**Phone:**
- International format
- Example: +41 22 791 21 11

**Address:**
- Headquarters or regional office
- Street, city, country
- Optional field

**4. Partnership Details**

**Partnership Start Date:**
- When partnership began
- For display and tracking

**Partnership Status:**
- Active
- Inactive
- Prospective

**Partnership Type:**
- Research Collaboration
- Funding Partnership
- Technical Assistance
- Capacity Building
- Joint Programming
- Advocacy
- Multiple types possible

**Focus Areas:**
- Health systems strengthening
- Research
- Training & education
- Policy development
- Service delivery
- Multiple selections allowed

**5. Social Media**

**Facebook:**
- Partner's Facebook page URL

**Twitter/X:**
- Twitter handle or URL

**LinkedIn:**
- LinkedIn company page URL

**6. Display Settings**

**Featured Partner:**
- Checkbox to feature on homepage
- Only 6-8 featured at a time
- Rotate periodically

**Display Order:**
- Number field
- Lower = appears first
- Group by importance or alphabetical

**Logo Display Size:**
- Small, Medium, Large
- Adjusts logo size on partner page

**7. Status & Visibility**

**Status:**
- Published - Visible on website
- Draft - Hidden, work in progress
- Archived - Former partner, preserved

### 18.3 Partners Datatable

**Columns:**
1. **Logo** - Thumbnail preview
2. **Partner Name** - Organization name (clickable)
3. **Type** - Partner type badge
4. **Website** - Link icon if URL provided
5. **Status** - Active/Inactive/Prospective
6. **Featured** - Star icon if featured
7. **Actions** - View, Edit, Delete

**Filters:**

**Type Filter:**
- All Types
- Academic Institutions
- Government Agencies
- NGOs
- International Organizations
- Funders
- Etc.

**Status Filter:**
- All
- Published
- Draft
- Archived

**Partnership Status Filter:**
- All
- Active
- Inactive
- Prospective

**Featured Filter:**
- All Partners
- Featured Only
- Non-Featured

**Sorting:**
- Name (A-Z, Z-A)
- Type
- Partnership Start Date
- Display Order

**Bulk Actions:**
- Delete selected
- Change status
- Feature/Unfeature
- Change type

### 18.4 Frontend Display

**Partners Page:**

**Layout Options:**
- Grid of logos
- List with descriptions
- Categorized by type

**Partner Card/Tile:**
- Logo (sized appropriately)
- Partner name
- Short description
- Link to website (optional)
- Click for more details

**Partner Detail Page (if enabled):**
- Large logo
- Full name
- Full description
- Contact information
- Website link
- Social media links
- Joint projects/publications
- Partnership timeline

**Featured Partners:**
- Homepage section
- Larger logos or special placement
- Links to partner page or website

### 18.5 Partner Management Best Practices

✅ **Logo Quality:**
- Request official brand assets
- High resolution (vector if possible)
- Transparent backgrounds for flexibility
- Consistent sizing across all logos
- Respect brand guidelines

✅ **Accurate Information:**
- Verify website URLs work
- Keep contact info current
- Update when partnerships change
- Archive inactive partnerships
- Regular annual review

✅ **Organization:**
- Logical grouping by type
- Featured partners rotated
- Alphabetical within types
- Most significant partners prominent

✅ **Permissions:**
- Get approval before listing
- Share draft for review
- Respect confidentiality
- Honor branding requirements

### 18.6 Common Workflows

**Workflow 1: Adding New Funder**

1. Create new partner
2. Name: "Gates Foundation"
3. Type: Funder/Donor
4. Upload logo (PNG with transparency)
5. Description: Partnership focus, funded projects
6. Website: https://www.gatesfoundation.org
7. Partnership Start Date: January 1, 2024
8. Status: Active
9. Feature partner: Yes
10. Display order: 1 (high priority)
11. Save and publish
12. Verify on partners page
13. Announce partnership via news article

**Workflow 2: Updating Partner Information**

1. Partner changes website URL
2. Navigate to Partners list
3. Find partner
4. Edit
5. Update website URL
6. Verify new URL works
7. Save
8. Test link on frontend

**Workflow 3: Archiving Ended Partnership**

1. Partnership concluded
2. Navigate to partner profile
3. Edit
4. Change Partnership Status: Inactive
5. Update description: "AHC partnered with [Organization] from 2020-2024..."
6. Option A: Archive (remove from display)
7. Option B: Keep published with "Former Partner" note
8. Save
9. Move to bottom of display order if keeping visible

## Chapter 19: Media Gallery Management

*Managing photos, videos, and media albums*

### 19.1 Media Gallery Overview

The Media Gallery stores and displays photos and videos from events, field work, programs, and activities.

**Media Types:**
- Photos (JPG, PNG, WebP)
- Videos (links to YouTube/Vimeo, or uploaded files)
- Albums/Collections

**Use Cases:**
- Event photo galleries
- Project documentation
- Impact stories (visual)
- Annual reports (photos)
- Social media content
- Website imagery

### 19.2 Uploading Media

**Navigation:**
1. Sidebar: Media > Media Gallery
2. Click "Upload Media" or "Add New"

**Upload Methods:**

**Method 1: Single Upload**
1. Click "Upload Media"
2. Select image from computer
3. Fill in details
4. Assign to album (optional)
5. Save

**Method 2: Bulk Upload**
1. Click "Bulk Upload"
2. Select multiple images (Ctrl+click or Shift+click)
3. All upload in parallel
4. Thumbnails appear as each completes
5. Edit details individually or bulk edit
6. Assign all to same album
7. Save all

**Method 3: Drag and Drop**
1. Open Media Gallery page
2. Drag images from file explorer
3. Drop into upload area
4. Upload begins automatically
5. Edit details after upload

**Supported Formats:**
- Images: JPG, JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF
- Videos: MP4 (if allowed), or embed links
- Max file size: 10MB per image (configurable)

### 19.3 Media Details

**Required Information:**

**Title:**
- Descriptive title
- Example: "Health Workshop Participants, Addis Ababa"

**Alt Text:**
- REQUIRED for accessibility
- Describe what's in the image
- Example: "Group photo of 30 workshop participants standing in front of AHC banner"

**Optional Information:**

**Caption:**
- Displays below image on frontend
- Photo credit, date, context
- Example: "Participants at the Health Systems Workshop, November 2025. Photo: John Doe"

**Description:**
- Internal notes
- Not shown to public
- Search keywords
- Event/project reference

**Date Taken:**
- When photo was taken (vs upload date)
- Used for chronological sorting

**Photographer:**
- Photo credit
- Name of photographer
- Organization

**Copyright:**
- All Rights Reserved
- Creative Commons
- Public Domain

**Location:**
- Where photo was taken
- City, country

**Tags:**
- Keywords for filtering
- Example: "workshop", "addis-ababa", "2025", "training"

### 19.4 Albums/Collections

**What are Albums?**

Albums group related photos together:
- Event galleries
- Project documentation
- Thematic collections
- Year-based archives

**Creating an Album:**

1. Navigate to Media > Albums
2. Click "Create Album"
3. **Album Name:** Example: "Health Systems Workshop 2025"
4. **Description:** Event details, date, location
5. **Cover Image:** Select photo to represent album
6. **Date:** Event or project date
7. **Status:** Published, Draft
8. **Visibility:** Public, Private
9. Save

**Adding Photos to Album:**

**During Upload:**
- Select album from dropdown when uploading
- Photo automatically added to album

**After Upload:**
1. Navigate to Media Gallery
2. Select photos (checkboxes)
3. Bulk Actions > "Add to Album"
4. Choose album
5. Apply

**Album Organization:**
- Drag to reorder photos within album
- Set album cover image
- Caption at album level

### 19.5 Media Datatable

**Columns:**
1. **Thumbnail** - Small preview
2. **Title** - Media title
3. **Type** - Image/Video icon
4. **Album** - Which album(s) it belongs to
5. **Date** - Upload date or date taken
6. **Size** - File size
7. **Dimensions** - Width × Height
8. **Actions** - View, Edit, Delete

**Filters:**

**Type Filter:**
- All Media
- Images Only
- Videos Only

**Album Filter:**
- All Media
- Unassigned (not in any album)
- Select specific album

**Date Filter:**
- Upload date range
- Date taken range

**Tag Filter:**
- Filter by tags

**Search:**
- Search title, caption, alt text, tags

**Bulk Actions:**
- Delete selected
- Add to album
- Remove from album
- Change visibility
- Download as ZIP

### 19.6 Frontend Display

**Media Gallery Page:**

**Layout Options:**

**Grid Layout:**
- Thumbnails in grid
- Masonry or fixed grid
- Hover shows title
- Click opens lightbox

**Album View:**
- Albums shown as cards
- Cover image + album name
- Click album to see all photos

**Filtering:**
- By album
- By tag
- By year
- Search

**Lightbox:**
- Click photo opens full-size
- Navigation arrows (previous/next)
- Shows caption, photo credit
- Download button (optional)
- Share on social media (optional)
- Close button (X)

**Individual Photo Page:**
- Large photo display
- Title, caption
- Date taken
- Photographer credit
- Related photos (same album)
- Download button (optional)

### 19.7 Video Management

**Embedded Videos:**

**YouTube/Vimeo (Recommended):**
1. Upload video to YouTube or Vimeo
2. In Media Gallery, click "Add Video"
3. Enter video URL
4. System fetches thumbnail and details
5. Add title, description
6. Assign to album
7. Save

**Benefits:**
- No storage used
- Automatic transcoding
- Player controls
- Closed captions support
- Analytics

**Uploaded Videos:**
1. If system allows direct upload
2. Select video file (MP4)
3. Max file size: 100MB (varies)
4. Upload progress bar
5. System may transcode
6. Add details
7. Save

**Video Details:**
- Title
- Description
- Duration (auto-detected)
- Thumbnail (auto-generated or upload custom)
- Album assignment
- Tags

### 19.8 Media Best Practices

✅ **Photo Quality:**
- High resolution (at least 1920px wide)
- Good lighting and composition
- In focus, properly exposed
- Compressed for web (< 1MB ideal)

✅ **Privacy & Permissions:**
- Get consent for photos of people
- Especially children
- Model releases for prominent individuals
- Respect cultural sensitivities

✅ **Accessibility:**
- Always add alt text
- Describe what's shown
- Include relevant context
- Don't leave blank

✅ **Organization:**
- Create albums immediately after events
- Tag consistently
- Use descriptive titles
- Include dates
- Archive old media

✅ **Storage Management:**
- Regularly review and delete duplicates
- Compress images before upload
- Archive very old media
- Monitor storage usage

✅ **Copyright:**
- Only upload images you have rights to
- Credit photographers
- Specify license clearly
- Respect attribution requirements

### 19.9 Common Workflows

**Workflow 1: Event Photo Gallery**

**Day of Event:**
1. Photograph event
2. Select best photos (30-50 max)
3. Edit/enhance photos
4. Compress for web
5. Rename files descriptively

**Upload Process:**
1. Create album: "Health Workshop November 2025"
2. Album description: Date, location, participants, purpose
3. Bulk upload all photos
4. Add alt text to each (describe what's shown)
5. Add caption with photo credit: "Photo: Jane Smith"
6. Tag: "workshop", "2025", "addis-ababa", "training"
7. Set album cover (best photo)
8. Publish album

**Promotion:**
1. Publish album
2. Create news article: "Photos: Health Systems Workshop"
3. Share on social media with link to gallery
4. Email participants with gallery link

**Workflow 2: Adding Stock Photos**

1. Download stock photo (properly licensed)
2. Upload to Media Gallery
3. Title: Descriptive name
4. Alt text: Describe image
5. Description: Note it's stock photo, license info
6. Copyright: License type
7. Tags: Relevant keywords
8. Don't assign to event albums (create "Stock Images" album)
9. Save

**Workflow 3: Annual Photo Archive**

**End of Year:**
1. Create album: "AHC 2025 Year in Photos"
2. Review all year's media
3. Select 50-100 best photos representing year
4. Add to year album
5. Order chronologically or thematically
6. Write album description (year summary)
7. Feature in annual report
8. Publish to website
9. Share via newsletter

## Chapter 20: Contact Messages Management

*Handling inquiries and communication from website visitors*

### 20.1 Contact Messages Overview

Contact messages are submissions from the website contact form. Visitors send inquiries, feedback, partnership requests, or other communications.

**Message Types:**

- General inquiries
- Partnership requests
- Media inquiries
- Event registration questions
- Technical support
- Feedback and suggestions
- Complaints

### 20.2 Viewing Contact Messages

**Navigation:**
1. Sidebar: Communications > Contact Messages
2. See all messages in datatable

**Message Datatable:**

**Columns:**
1. **ID** - Message number
2. **Name** - Sender's name
3. **Email** - Sender's email (clickable to compose)
4. **Subject** - Message subject/topic
5. **Status** - New, Read, Replied (colored badges)
6. **Date** - When submitted
7. **Actions** - View, Delete

**Status Badges:**
- **New** - Green badge (unread, urgent)
- **Read** - Yellow badge (opened but not replied)
- **Replied** - Gray badge (response sent)

### 20.3 Message Details

**Viewing a Message:**

1. Click message in list
2. Opens detail page showing:

**Sender Information:**
- Full name
- Email address (clickable mailto link)
- Phone number (if provided)
- Organization/Affiliation (if provided)

**Message Content:**
- Subject line
- Full message text
- Submission date and time
- IP address (for security)
- User agent (browser/device)

**Message Metadata:**
- Submission timestamp
- Status (New/Read/Replied)
- Replied at timestamp (if replied)
- Who replied (user who marked as replied)

**Actions:**
- Email sender (opens email client)
- Toggle Replied status
- Delete message
- Print message
- Export message

### 20.4 Toggle Replied Status (NEW FEATURE)

The toggle replied feature allows you to mark messages as replied or not replied easily.

**How It Works:**

**When Status is New or Read:**
- Button shows: "Mark as Replied" (green)
- Click to mark message as replied
- Status changes to "Replied"
- Timestamp recorded
- Success message: "Message marked as replied"

**When Status is Replied:**
- Button shows: "Mark as Not Replied" (gray)
- Click to revert to read status
- Status changes to "Read"
- Replied timestamp cleared
- Success message: "Message marked as not replied"

**Use Cases:**

**Marking as Replied:**
1. View new message
2. Respond to sender via email
3. Return to message detail page
4. Click "Mark as Replied"
5. Message marked in system
6. Moves to "Replied" filter view

**Unmarking (if mistake):**
1. Realized you marked wrong message
2. Or response bounced/failed
3. Click "Mark as Not Replied"
4. Status reverts to "Read"
5. Message appears in "needs response" view again

**Benefits:**
- Track which messages need responses
- Know who responded to what
- Accountability
- Follow-up reminders
- Reporting (response rate, time to respond)

### 20.5 Filters and Search

**Status Filter:**
- All Messages
- New (unread, highest priority)
- Read (opened but not replied)
- Replied (completed)

**Date Range Filter:**
- Today
- Last 7 Days
- Last 30 Days
- This Month
- Custom range

**Search:**
- Search by name
- Search by email
- Search by subject
- Search message content

**Quick Filters:**
- Unread (New status)
- Needs Reply (New + Read)
- This Week
- Starred/Important (if flagging enabled)

### 20.6 Responding to Messages

**Response Workflow:**

**Method 1: Email Client (Recommended)**
1. View message in backend
2. Click sender's email address
3. Email client opens (Outlook, Gmail, etc.)
4. "To" field pre-filled
5. Write response
6. Send email
7. Return to backend
8. Click "Mark as Replied"

**Method 2: Reply Within System (if available)**
1. View message
2. Click "Reply" button
3. Compose response in form
4. CC/BCC options
5. Attach files (if needed)
6. Send
7. Automatically marks as replied
8. Copy saved in system

**Response Best Practices:**

✅ **Timely:**
- Respond within 24-48 hours
- Acknowledge receipt quickly
- Provide timeline if detailed answer needed

✅ **Professional:**
- Greeting with their name
- Answer all questions asked
- Provide relevant links/resources
- Clear next steps
- Professional signature

✅ **Accurate:**
- Verify information before responding
- Link to official resources
- CC relevant team members
- Follow up if promised

### 20.7 Message Management

**Bulk Actions:**

**Select Multiple Messages:**
- Checkboxes for each message
- Bulk actions dropdown appears

**Available Bulk Actions:**
- Delete selected
- Mark all as read
- Mark all as replied
- Export selected

**Deleting Messages:**

**Single Delete:**
1. View message
2. Click Delete button
3. Confirmation: "Are you sure?"
4. Confirm
5. Message permanently deleted

**Bulk Delete:**
1. Select multiple messages
2. Bulk Actions > Delete
3. Confirmation
4. All selected deleted

⚠️ **Warning:** Deletion is permanent. Consider archiving instead if you might need records.

**Archiving (if available):**
- Some systems support archiving
- Moves message out of active inbox
- Still searchable, not deleted
- Can restore if needed

### 20.8 Message Notifications

**Notification Settings:**

**Email Notifications:**
1. Navigate to Settings > Notifications
2. Contact Message Notifications section
3. Options:
   - Instant (email for each new message)
   - Daily digest (summary once per day)
   - Weekly digest
   - Disabled

**Who Gets Notified:**
- Users with "View Contact Messages" permission
- Super Administrators (always)
- Designated contact manager
- Custom notification rules

**Notification Content:**
- Sender name and email
- Subject line
- First 100 characters of message
- Link to view in backend
- "Reply Now" button

### 20.9 Contact Message Analytics

**Metrics:**

**Volume:**
- Total messages received
- By time period (day, week, month)
- Trend over time

**Response Metrics:**
- Average response time
- % replied vs. unanswered
- Response rate by team member

**Message Categories:**
- Breakdown by subject/type
- Common topics
- Frequently asked questions

**Reports:**

1. Navigate to Communications > Contact Reports
2. Select date range
3. View metrics
4. Export to CSV/PDF
5. Use for:
   - Performance tracking
   - FAQ development
   - Staffing decisions
   - Website improvements

### 20.10 Best Practices

✅ **Daily Review:**
- Check messages at least once daily
- Prioritize new/urgent messages
- Respond promptly to simple inquiries
- Escalate complex issues to appropriate staff

✅ **Template Responses:**
- Create templates for common questions
- FAQ answers
- Partnership information
- Event details
- Customize before sending

✅ **Team Coordination:**
- Assign messages to team members
- Use notes/comments for internal communication
- Don't duplicate responses
- Share workload

✅ **Privacy:**
- Don't share sender info without permission
- Secure personal data
- Comply with privacy policies
- Delete old messages per retention policy

✅ **Follow-Up:**
- Set reminders for promised follow-ups
- Check if issues resolved
- Request feedback
- Build relationships

### 20.11 Common Workflows

**Workflow 1: Daily Message Review**

**Morning Routine:**
1. Login to admin panel
2. Navigate to Contact Messages
3. Filter: New + Read (needs response)
4. Sort by date (oldest first)
5. Review each message:
   - Simple inquiry → respond immediately via email → mark replied
   - Partnership request → forward to director → mark read
   - Media inquiry → respond with media kit → mark replied
   - Technical issue → forward to IT → mark read
6. Check all marked as replied
7. Document any follow-ups needed

**Workflow 2: Partnership Inquiry**

**Message Received:**
- Subject: "Partnership Opportunity"
- From: NGO Director
- Requesting collaboration on health project

**Response Process:**
1. View message in detail
2. Forward to Partnership Director
3. Add note: "Sent to Sarah for review"
4. Mark as Read (not yet replied)
5. Partnership Director reviews
6. Drafts partnership response
7. Sends email to inquirer
8. Returns to contact message
9. Marks as Replied
10. Adds note: "Sent partnership info package"

**Workflow 3: Weekly Message Summary**

**End of Week:**
1. Navigate to Contact Reports
2. Select: Last 7 days
3. Review metrics:
   - 23 messages received
   - 21 replied (91% response rate)
   - Average response time: 6 hours
   - Top topics: Event inquiries (8), General info (7), Partnership (5), Technical (3)
4. Export report
5. Share with management
6. Identify trends (e.g., many event questions → update event page FAQ)

**Workflow 4: Handling Spam**

**Identify Spam:**
- Generic subject: "Business proposal", "Opportunity"
- Sender email: Random/suspicious
- Message: Generic template, irrelevant offer
- No specific AHC mention

**Action:**
1. Mark as spam (if feature available)
2. Delete message
3. Don't respond (encourages more spam)
4. If persistent, block sender
5. Report to Super Admin if excessive

## Chapter 21: Newsletter Subscribers Management

### 21.1 Overview

The Newsletter Subscribers module manages your email subscription list for AHC newsletters, updates, and announcements. This system helps you build and maintain an engaged audience, send targeted communications, and comply with email marketing regulations.

**Key Features:**
- Subscriber list management
- Import/Export functionality
- Subscription status tracking
- Opt-in/Opt-out management
- Segmentation capabilities
- Compliance with email marketing laws
- Integration with newsletter sending systems
- Subscriber analytics

**Access:**
```
Admin Panel → Communications → Newsletter Subscribers
```

**Required Permission:** Manage Newsletter Subscribers

### 21.2 Subscriber Datatable

**Overview:**

The subscriber datatable displays all newsletter subscribers with filtering, search, and management capabilities.

**Columns Displayed:**

| Column | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| Email | Subscriber's email address |
| Name | Subscriber's name (if provided) |
| Status | Active / Inactive / Unsubscribed |
| Source | Where they subscribed (Website, Event, Manual) |
| Subscribed Date | Date they joined the list |
| Last Sent | Last newsletter sent to this subscriber |
| Actions | View / Edit / Delete / Export |

**Filters Available:**

**Status Filter:**
- All Subscribers
- Active (currently subscribed)
- Inactive (paused subscriptions)
- Unsubscribed (opted out)

**Source Filter:**
- All Sources
- Website Footer Form
- Event Registration
- Manual Entry
- Import

**Date Range Filter:**
- Last 7 days
- Last 30 days
- Last 90 days
- Custom date range

**Search:**
- Search by email address
- Search by name
- Real-time filtering

**Bulk Actions:**
- Export selected subscribers
- Mark as Active/Inactive
- Delete selected
- Add to segment

### 21.3 Adding Subscribers Manually

**When to Add Manually:**
- Event attendees who opted in on paper forms
- Business cards collected at conferences
- Phone requests to join mailing list
- Corrections/Updates needed

**Steps:**

1. Navigate to Newsletter Subscribers
2. Click "Add New Subscriber" button
3. Complete the form:

**Required Fields:**
```
Email Address: [text input]
  ├─ Valid email format required
  ├─ Check for duplicates automatically
  └─ Example: john.doe@example.com

Name: [text input]
  ├─ First and last name
  └─ Example: John Doe
```

**Optional Fields:**
```
Phone Number: [text input]
Organization: [text input]
Source: [dropdown]
  ├─ Website
  ├─ Event Registration
  ├─ Manual Entry
  ├─ Import
  └─ Other

Opt-in Method: [dropdown]
  ├─ Double Opt-in (email confirmation)
  ├─ Single Opt-in (direct signup)
  └─ Manual (verbal consent)

Notes: [textarea]
  └─ Internal notes about subscriber
```

4. Select Status:
   - Active (will receive newsletters)
   - Inactive (won't receive, but not unsubscribed)

5. Click "Save Subscriber"

**Important:** Always have documented consent before adding someone manually to comply with email marketing regulations (CAN-SPAM, GDPR, etc.).

### 21.4 Importing Subscribers

**Bulk Import:**

Import multiple subscribers from CSV file:

**Step 1: Prepare CSV File**

Create CSV with these columns:
```
email,name,phone,organization,source,opt_in_date
john.doe@example.com,John Doe,+1234567890,ABC Corp,Event,2025-12-01
jane.smith@example.com,Jane Smith,,XYZ Org,Website,2025-11-15
```

**Required Column:**
- email

**Optional Columns:**
- name
- phone
- organization
- source
- opt_in_date

**Step 2: Import Process**

1. Click "Import Subscribers" button
2. Select your CSV file
3. Map CSV columns to database fields:
   ```
   CSV Column          →  Database Field
   ─────────────────────  ────────────────
   email               →  Email Address
   name                →  Full Name
   phone               →  Phone Number
   organization        →  Organization
   source              →  Subscription Source
   opt_in_date         →  Opt-in Date
   ```

4. Review import settings:
   - Skip duplicates (recommended)
   - Update existing subscribers
   - Mark all as Active/Inactive

5. Preview first 10 rows
6. Click "Import"
7. Review import summary:
   - Successfully imported: 45
   - Duplicates skipped: 3
   - Errors: 2 (invalid email format)

**Step 3: Post-Import Verification**

1. Filter by source = "Import"
2. Filter by today's date
3. Verify subscriber count matches expected
4. Spot-check several entries for accuracy
5. Send welcome email to new subscribers (optional)

### 21.5 Managing Subscriber Status

**Status Types:**

**Active:**
- Currently subscribed
- Will receive all newsletters
- Can opt-out anytime
- Most subscribers should be in this status

**Inactive:**
- Temporarily paused
- Won't receive newsletters
- Not unsubscribed (can be reactivated)
- Use for: bounced emails, temporary holds

**Unsubscribed:**
- Opted out permanently
- Cannot be reactivated without new consent
- Required by law to honor
- Keep in database for suppression list

**Changing Status:**

**Individual Subscriber:**
1. Find subscriber in datatable
2. Click "Edit" action
3. Change Status dropdown
4. Add note explaining reason (optional)
5. Click "Update Subscriber"

**Bulk Status Change:**
1. Select multiple subscribers (checkboxes)
2. Click "Bulk Actions" dropdown
3. Choose:
   - Mark as Active
   - Mark as Inactive
   - Mark as Unsubscribed
4. Confirm action
5. Status updated for all selected

**Automatic Status Changes:**

System automatically updates status:
- Email bounces (hard bounce) → Inactive
- User clicks "Unsubscribe" link → Unsubscribed
- Multiple soft bounces → Inactive

### 21.6 Unsubscribe Management

**Honoring Unsubscribe Requests:**

When someone clicks "Unsubscribe" in a newsletter:

1. System automatically:
   - Changes status to "Unsubscribed"
   - Records unsubscribe date
   - Displays confirmation message to user
   - Sends confirmation email (optional)

2. Unsubscribed users:
   - Never receive newsletters again
   - Remain in database as suppression list
   - Cannot be manually reactivated (legal requirement)
   - Must complete new opt-in form to resubscribe

**Manual Unsubscribe:**

If someone requests unsubscribe via phone/email:

1. Find their record in datatable
2. Click "Edit"
3. Change Status to "Unsubscribed"
4. Add note: "Requested via email on [date]"
5. Click "Update Subscriber"
6. Send confirmation email
7. Document the request

**Compliance:**

✅ **Must Do:**
- Include unsubscribe link in every newsletter
- Process unsubscribes immediately (within 10 business days legally, but instantly is best practice)
- Never email unsubscribed contacts
- Keep unsubscribe records

❌ **Never Do:**
- Remove unsubscribe link
- Make unsubscribe process difficult
- Reactivate unsubscribed contacts without new consent
- Share unsubscribe list with third parties

### 21.7 Exporting Subscribers

**Export Options:**

**Full Export:**
1. Click "Export All" button
2. Select format:
   - CSV (for spreadsheets)
   - Excel (.xlsx)
   - PDF (for printing)
3. Choose fields to include:
   - Email (always included)
   - Name
   - Phone
   - Organization
   - Status
   - Subscribe Date
   - Source
4. Click "Download"
5. File downloads to your computer

**Filtered Export:**
1. Apply filters (status, date range, source)
2. Click "Export Filtered Results"
3. Only filtered subscribers exported
4. Useful for:
   - Active subscribers only (for newsletter sending)
   - Specific time period
   - Specific source

**Selective Export:**
1. Select specific subscribers (checkboxes)
2. Click "Bulk Actions" → "Export Selected"
3. Only checked subscribers exported

**Use Cases:**

- **Email Service Provider Import:** Export active subscribers to import into Mailchimp, SendGrid, etc.
- **Event Invitations:** Export segment for targeted event promotion
- **Reports:** Include in monthly communications reports
- **Backup:** Regular backup of subscriber list
- **Compliance:** Provide data export if requested by subscriber (GDPR right to data portability)

### 21.8 Subscriber Analytics

**Key Metrics:**

**Growth Metrics:**
```
Total Subscribers: 1,247
  ├─ Active: 1,156 (92.7%)
  ├─ Inactive: 54 (4.3%)
  └─ Unsubscribed: 37 (3.0%)

Growth Rate:
  ├─ New subscribers this month: +87
  ├─ Unsubscribes this month: -12
  └─ Net growth: +75 (6.5% growth)

Growth Trend (Last 6 Months):
  Jan: +45, Feb: +67, Mar: +52, Apr: +78, May: +91, Jun: +87
```

**Source Breakdown:**
```
Subscription Sources:
  ├─ Website Footer Form: 687 (55%)
  ├─ Event Registration: 312 (25%)
  ├─ Manual Entry: 156 (12.5%)
  └─ Import: 92 (7.5%)
```

**Engagement Metrics:**
- Open rate (if integrated with email service)
- Click-through rate
- Most engaged subscribers
- Least engaged (consider re-engagement campaign)

**Viewing Analytics:**

1. Navigate to Newsletter Subscribers
2. Click "Analytics" tab
3. Select date range
4. View charts and graphs:
   - Subscriber growth over time (line chart)
   - Source breakdown (pie chart)
   - Status distribution (bar chart)
   - Monthly new subscribers vs. unsubscribes (comparison chart)

### 21.9 Segmentation

**What is Segmentation?**

Grouping subscribers based on criteria to send targeted newsletters.

**Segment Examples:**

**Geographic Segments:**
- Ethiopia-based subscribers
- International subscribers
- Regional groups

**Interest-Based Segments:**
- Health professionals
- Students and researchers
- Partner organizations
- General public

**Engagement-Based Segments:**
- Highly engaged (opens every email)
- Moderately engaged (opens some emails)
- Low engagement (rarely opens)
- New subscribers (joined last 30 days)

**Source-Based Segments:**
- Event attendees
- Website signups
- Imported contacts

**Creating a Segment:**

1. Click "Create Segment" button
2. Name your segment: "Health Professionals"
3. Add criteria:
   ```
   Organization contains: Hospital, Clinic, Health Center
   OR
   Email domain contains: @moh.gov.et
   ```
4. Preview matching subscribers
5. Save segment
6. Use segment for targeted newsletters

**Using Segments:**

When sending newsletters (via integrated email service):
1. Select segment instead of "All Active Subscribers"
2. Customize message for that audience
3. Send targeted communication
4. Track segment-specific engagement

### 21.10 Best Practices

✅ **Consent and Compliance:**
- Always obtain clear consent before adding someone
- Use double opt-in when possible (send confirmation email)
- Include unsubscribe link in every communication
- Honor unsubscribe requests immediately
- Keep records of consent
- Comply with CAN-SPAM Act, GDPR, and other regulations

✅ **List Hygiene:**
- Regularly clean your list:
  - Remove hard bounces
  - Remove inactive subscribers after 12+ months of no engagement
  - Verify large imports before sending
- Run re-engagement campaigns before removing inactive subscribers
- Monitor bounce rates
- Update subscriber information when provided

✅ **Data Security:**
- Protect subscriber data
- Limit access to authorized staff only
- Don't share list with third parties without consent
- Use secure methods for exports
- Regular backups

✅ **Engagement:**
- Send valuable content regularly (but not too often)
- Segment your list for targeted messages
- Personalize when possible
- Monitor open rates and adjust strategy
- Ask for feedback
- Make it easy to update preferences

✅ **Growth Strategies:**
- Prominent signup form on website
- Offer incentive (e.g., "Subscribe for AHC health tips")
- Collect at events
- Social media promotion
- Make value proposition clear
- Use double opt-in to ensure quality

### 21.11 Common Workflows

**Workflow 1: Weekly List Maintenance**

**Every Monday Morning:**
1. Navigate to Newsletter Subscribers
2. Filter: Status = Inactive
3. Review inactive subscribers:
   - Check bounce reason
   - Verify if email address is typo
   - Remove hard bounces permanently
   - Note patterns (e.g., all from certain domain)
4. Filter: Status = Active, Last Sent > 6 months ago
5. Identify low-engagement subscribers
6. Plan re-engagement campaign
7. Export active list for this week's newsletter
8. Review growth metrics
9. Update management report

**Workflow 2: Post-Event Subscriber Import**

**After Health Conference:**
- 87 people signed up on paper form

**Process:**
1. Create CSV from paper forms:
   - Double-check email addresses (ask if handwriting unclear)
   - Include source: "2025 Annual Health Conference"
   - Include opt-in date: Conference date
2. Import CSV to Newsletter Subscribers
3. Map fields correctly
4. Preview import
5. Complete import
6. Review summary: 87 imported, 3 duplicates skipped
7. Create segment: "2025 Conference Attendees"
8. Send targeted welcome email:
   - Thank them for attending
   - Include conference resources
   - Set expectations for newsletter frequency
9. Update event report with subscriber count

**Workflow 3: Monthly Newsletter Send**

**Preparation:**
1. Navigate to Newsletter Subscribers
2. Filter: Status = Active
3. Export active subscribers
4. Import to email service (e.g., Mailchimp)
5. Create newsletter content
6. Send test email to staff
7. Review and approve
8. Schedule/Send newsletter
9. Monitor delivery and engagement
10. Return to AHC admin panel
11. Update "Last Sent" date for subscribers (if not automatic)
12. Note open rate and clicks in analytics

**Workflow 4: Handling Unsubscribe Request via Email**

**Received Email:**
"Please remove me from your mailing list - jane.smith@example.com"

**Response Process:**
1. Navigate to Newsletter Subscribers
2. Search: jane.smith@example.com
3. Click "Edit"
4. Change Status: Unsubscribed
5. Add Note: "Unsubscribe requested via email on 2025-12-10"
6. Click "Update Subscriber"
7. Reply to Jane's email:
   ```
   Dear Jane,

   Your email address has been removed from our newsletter list.
   You will not receive any future newsletters from AHC.

   If this was a mistake, you can resubscribe at: [signup URL]

   Best regards,
   AHC Communications Team
   ```
8. Document in communications log

## Chapter 22: User Management

### 22.1 Overview

The User Management module controls who can access the AHC admin panel and what they can do. This includes creating user accounts, assigning roles and permissions, managing user status, and monitoring user activity.

**Key Concepts:**

- **User:** Individual with login credentials
- **Role:** Group of permissions (e.g., Super Admin, Editor, Moderator)
- **Permission:** Specific action ability (e.g., Edit News, Delete Events)
- **Status:** Active, Inactive, Suspended

**Access:**
```
Admin Panel → System → User Management
```

**Required Permission:** Manage Users (typically Super Admin only)

### 22.2 User Roles Explained

**Super Admin:**
- Highest level of access
- Can do everything in the system
- Manage all users and roles
- Access system settings
- Cannot be deleted by other admins
- Typically: Director, IT Manager

**Permissions:** All permissions enabled

**Admin:**
- High level of access
- Manage content and users (except Super Admins)
- Cannot modify system settings
- Cannot change own role
- Typically: Deputy Director, Senior Staff

**Permissions:** Most permissions except critical system settings

**Editor:**
- Content management focus
- Create, edit, publish content
- Manage media gallery
- Cannot manage users or settings
- Typically: Content Manager, Communications Officer

**Permissions:**
- Manage News & Announcements
- Manage Events
- Manage Dynamic Pages
- Manage Media Gallery
- Manage AHC Leaders & Teams
- Manage Partners
- View Contact Messages

**Moderator:**
- Content review and moderation
- Approve/reject submitted content
- Respond to messages
- Cannot create major content
- Typically: Junior Staff, Interns

**Permissions:**
- View all content
- Edit existing content
- Manage Contact Messages
- Manage Newsletter Subscribers
- Cannot delete or create new content

**Viewer:**
- Read-only access
- View analytics and reports
- Cannot modify anything
- Typically: Board Members, Consultants

**Permissions:**
- View Dashboard
- View Analytics
- View Reports
- No edit/delete permissions

### 22.3 User Datatable

**Columns Displayed:**

| Column | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| Name | User's full name |
| Email | Login email address |
| Role | Assigned role (Super Admin, Editor, etc.) |
| Status | Active / Inactive / Suspended |
| Last Login | Last access date/time |
| Created | Account creation date |
| Actions | View / Edit / Suspend / Delete |

**Filters:**

**Role Filter:**
- All Roles
- Super Admin
- Admin
- Editor
- Moderator
- Viewer

**Status Filter:**
- All Statuses
- Active
- Inactive
- Suspended

**Search:**
- Search by name
- Search by email
- Real-time filtering

**Sorting:**
- Sort by name (A-Z, Z-A)
- Sort by last login (newest, oldest)
- Sort by role
- Sort by creation date

### 22.4 Creating a New User

**When to Create Users:**
- New staff member joins
- External consultant needs access
- Intern or volunteer assigned tasks
- Partnership coordinator from partner organization

**Steps:**

1. Navigate to User Management
2. Click "Add New User" button
3. Complete the registration form:

**Personal Information:**
```
Full Name: [text input]
  └─ Example: Dr. Alemayehu Bekele

Email Address: [text input]
  ├─ Will be used for login
  ├─ Must be unique
  └─ Example: alemayehu.bekele@ahc.org

Phone Number: [text input] (optional)
  └─ Example: +251 91 123 4567

Profile Photo: [file upload] (optional)
  ├─ JPG or PNG
  ├─ Max 2MB
  └─ Recommended: 400x400px
```

**Account Settings:**
```
Role: [dropdown]
  ├─ Super Admin
  ├─ Admin
  ├─ Editor
  ├─ Moderator
  └─ Viewer

Status: [radio buttons]
  ├─ Active (can login immediately)
  └─ Inactive (account created but login disabled)

Language Preference: [dropdown]
  ├─ English
  └─ Amharic
```

**Password Settings:**
```
Password: [password input]
  ├─ Minimum 8 characters
  ├─ Must include uppercase, lowercase, number
  └─ Special characters recommended

Confirm Password: [password input]
  └─ Must match password

Send Welcome Email: [checkbox]
  ├─ Checked: User receives email with login details
  └─ Unchecked: Manually provide credentials
```

**Permissions (if custom role):**
```
☐ Select All Permissions

Content Management:
  ☐ Manage News & Announcements
  ☐ Manage Events
  ☐ Manage Dynamic Pages
  ☐ Manage Resources

Community:
  ☐ Manage AHC Leaders & Teams
  ☐ Manage Partners
  ☐ Manage Media Gallery

Communications:
  ☐ Manage Contact Messages
  ☐ Manage Newsletter Subscribers

System:
  ☐ Manage Users
  ☐ Manage Roles
  ☐ System Settings
  ☐ View Analytics
```

4. Click "Create User"
5. Success message appears
6. User can now login (if Active)

**Welcome Email Content:**

If "Send Welcome Email" is checked, user receives:
```
Subject: Welcome to AHC Admin Panel

Dear [Name],

Your account has been created for the African Health Catalysts admin panel.

Login Details:
  Email: [user.email]
  Password: [temporary password]
  Login URL: https://ahc.org/admin

Your Role: [Editor]

Please login and change your password immediately for security.

If you have questions, contact: [admin.email]

Best regards,
AHC Admin Team
```

### 22.5 Editing User Details

**When to Edit:**
- User changes email address
- Role promotion/demotion
- Update contact information
- Modify permissions
- Update profile photo

**Steps:**

1. Find user in datatable
2. Click "Edit" action
3. Modify desired fields:
   - Personal information
   - Role assignment
   - Status
   - Permissions
4. Click "Update User"
5. Changes take effect immediately

**Important Notes:**
- Users cannot change their own role (security)
- Super Admins cannot be edited by regular Admins
- Active sessions continue until user logs out and back in (for permission changes to fully apply)
- Email changes require re-verification (optional security feature)

### 22.6 User Status Management

**Status Types:**

**Active:**
- Can login and access system
- All permissions functional
- Normal operational status

**Inactive:**
- Cannot login
- Account exists but disabled
- Temporary hold
- Use for: Staff on leave, pending approval

**Suspended:**
- Cannot login
- Account flagged for security/policy violation
- Requires investigation before reactivation
- Use for: Security concerns, policy breaches

**Changing Status:**

1. Find user in datatable
2. Click "Edit" or quick status toggle
3. Select new status
4. Add reason note (recommended):
   ```
   Reason: On medical leave until Jan 2026
   ```
5. Click "Update"
6. User's access immediately affected

**Suspended User:**
- Receives email notification (optional)
- Cannot login (sees "Account suspended" message)
- Contact admin for reactivation
- Investigate before reactivating

### 22.7 Resetting User Passwords

**When Needed:**
- User forgets password
- Security breach suspected
- First login password change
- Routine password rotation

**Admin-Initiated Reset:**

1. Find user in datatable
2. Click "Actions" → "Reset Password"
3. Choose method:

**Method A: Auto-Generate**
- System creates random secure password
- Email sent to user automatically
- User must change on first login

**Method B: Manual Set**
- Admin enters new password
- Admin provides to user securely (not via email)
- User must change on first login

4. Click "Reset Password"
5. Confirmation shown
6. User receives email (if auto-generate)

**User Self-Service Reset:**

From login page, users can:
1. Click "Forgot Password?"
2. Enter email address
3. Receive reset link via email
4. Click link (valid for 60 minutes)
5. Enter new password
6. Login with new password

**Security Best Practices:**
- Expire passwords every 90 days (optional policy)
- Require complex passwords
- Don't send passwords via regular email
- Log all password reset attempts
- Lock account after 5 failed login attempts

### 22.8 Deleting Users

**When to Delete:**
- Staff permanently left organization
- Duplicate account
- External consultant project completed
- Account created by mistake

**Before Deleting:**

✅ **Check:**
- Is user still with organization?
- Any content created by this user?
- Any active tasks assigned?
- Alternative: Set to Inactive instead?

**Soft Delete vs. Hard Delete:**

**Soft Delete (Recommended):**
- User marked as deleted
- Remains in database
- Content preserved
- Can be restored if needed
- Audit trail maintained

**Hard Delete (Permanent):**
- User completely removed
- Cannot be undone
- Content may be orphaned
- Use with extreme caution

**Deletion Process:**

1. Navigate to User Management
2. Find user in datatable
3. Click "Delete" action
4. Confirmation dialog:
   ```
   Delete User: John Doe?

   This user has created:
   - 12 News Articles
   - 5 Events
   - 8 Media Uploads

   Content will be preserved (attributed to "Deleted User").

   Are you sure? [Cancel] [Delete]
   ```
5. Click "Delete" to confirm
6. User removed from system
7. Login credentials no longer valid

**Cannot Delete:**
- Your own account (must be done by another admin)
- Last Super Admin (system protection)
- Users with active sessions (must logout first, or force logout)

### 22.9 Monitoring User Activity

**Activity Logs:**

Track what users do in the system for security and accountability.

**Viewing Activity Logs:**

```
Admin Panel → System → Activity Logs
```

**Log Entries:**

| Timestamp | User | Action | Details | IP Address |
|-----------|------|--------|---------|------------|
| 2025-12-10 14:32 | John Doe | Created Event | "Annual Health Symposium" | 192.168.1.45 |
| 2025-12-10 14:15 | Jane Smith | Edited News | "Updated COVID-19 article" | 10.0.0.12 |
| 2025-12-10 13:58 | Admin | Deleted Media | "old-logo.png" | 192.168.1.10 |

**Filterable by:**
- User (dropdown)
- Action type (Create, Edit, Delete, Login, Logout)
- Date range
- Module (News, Events, Users, etc.)

**Login History:**

```
User → View Profile → Login History Tab
```

Shows:
- Login date/time
- IP address
- Location (if available)
- Device/Browser
- Session duration
- Logout type (manual, timeout, forced)

**Security Alerts:**

Monitor for suspicious activity:
- Multiple failed login attempts
- Login from unusual location
- Login at unusual time
- Rapid succession of delete actions
- Permission changes
- User account modifications

### 22.10 Best Practices

✅ **Principle of Least Privilege:**
- Give users minimum permissions needed for their job
- Don't make everyone Admin
- Review permissions quarterly
- Remove permissions when responsibilities change

✅ **Account Security:**
- Strong password requirements
- Two-factor authentication (if available)
- Regular password changes
- Lock accounts after failed login attempts
- Monitor login activity

✅ **Onboarding Process:**
1. Create account with appropriate role
2. Send welcome email with login instructions
3. Schedule initial training session
4. Provide user guide
5. Assign mentor for first week
6. Review after 30 days

✅ **Offboarding Process:**
1. Notification received: User leaving on [date]
2. One week before: Backup user's content
3. Last day: Change status to Inactive
4. Two weeks after: If no issues, delete account
5. Document handover of responsibilities
6. Update contact lists

✅ **Regular Audits:**
- Monthly: Review active users (are they all still staff?)
- Quarterly: Review permissions (still appropriate?)
- Annually: Full security audit
- Remove inactive accounts after 90 days
- Document all changes

✅ **Communication:**
- Notify users when accounts created
- Inform users of role/permission changes
- Explain suspensions clearly
- Provide support for login issues
- Maintain updated contact list

### 22.11 Common Workflows

**Workflow 1: New Staff Member Onboarding**

**Scenario:** New Communications Officer starts Monday

**Friday Before Start:**
1. Receive onboarding request from HR
2. Navigate to User Management
3. Click "Add New User"
4. Fill in details:
   - Name: Sarah Johnson
   - Email: sarah.johnson@ahc.org
   - Phone: +251 91 234 5678
   - Role: Editor
   - Status: Active
   - Password: Auto-generate
   - Send Welcome Email: ✅ Checked
5. Click "Create User"
6. Verify welcome email sent
7. Prepare training materials
8. Add to staff communication channels

**Monday Morning:**
1. Meet Sarah for orientation
2. Help with first login
3. Guide through password change
4. Tour admin panel features
5. Assign first task (create test news article)
6. Provide user guide
7. Schedule follow-up for Wednesday

**Wednesday Follow-up:**
1. Review Sarah's progress
2. Answer questions
3. Adjust permissions if needed
4. Confirm comfortable with system

**Workflow 2: Staff Member Leaving Organization**

**Scenario:** Editor leaving at end of month

**Two Weeks Before:**
1. Notification from HR: John Doe leaving Dec 31
2. Navigate to User Management
3. Find John Doe
4. Review his content:
   - Created 45 news articles
   - Uploaded 120 media files
   - Managed 15 events
5. Assign replacement: Sarah will take over
6. Export John's activity report
7. Document knowledge transfer plan

**Last Week:**
1. Knowledge transfer sessions with Sarah
2. Update content ownership (if needed)
3. Backup John's work
4. Retrieve any special access credentials

**Last Day (Dec 31):**
1. Navigate to User Management
2. Find John Doe
3. Click "Edit"
4. Change Status: Inactive
5. Add note: "Left organization Dec 31, 2025"
6. Click "Update User"
7. John can no longer login
8. Send exit email confirming account disabled

**Two Weeks Later (Jan 14):**
1. Verify no issues with content
2. Confirm no access needed
3. Navigate to User Management
4. Find John Doe (filter: Inactive)
5. Click "Delete"
6. Confirm deletion
7. Update user count in reports

**Workflow 3: Security Incident - Compromised Account**

**Alert:** Unusual login activity detected for user "jane.smith@ahc.org"

**Immediate Action (within minutes):**
1. Navigate to User Management
2. Find Jane Smith
3. Click "Edit"
4. Change Status: Suspended
5. Click "Update User" (Jane immediately logged out)
6. Call Jane: "Did you just login from Nigeria?"
7. Jane: "No, I'm in Addis Ababa and haven't logged in today"
8. Confirmed: Account compromised

**Investigation:**
1. Navigate to Activity Logs
2. Filter: User = Jane Smith, Last 48 hours
3. Review suspicious activities:
   - Login from Nigeria IP: 197.234.xxx.xxx
   - 15 failed login attempts before success
   - Downloaded user database
   - Attempted to create Admin user
4. Document all findings
5. Check other accounts for similar activity

**Remediation:**
1. Reset Jane's password
2. Review and revoke any suspicious changes
3. Check for data exfiltration
4. Notify IT/Security team
5. Review system logs
6. Implement additional security (2FA, IP restrictions)

**Recovery:**
1. Call Jane to explain situation
2. Generate new secure password
3. Provide to Jane via secure channel (phone, in-person)
4. Navigate to User Management
5. Find Jane Smith
6. Click "Edit"
7. Change Status: Active
8. Add note: "Reactivated after security incident - 2025-12-10"
9. Click "Update User"
10. Jane can login with new password
11. Verify Jane's access working
12. Monitor for 48 hours
13. Document incident in security log

**Prevention:**
1. Implement 2FA for all users
2. IP whitelisting for admin panel
3. Security training for all staff
4. Regular password changes
5. Monitor login anomalies
